###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.13 — Mechanize the visibility policy and remove the book's duplicated frontier

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0061, found during the 2026-09-11 PNT resumption's book read.
- Reproduce: a tracked-Markdown census finds the superseded private-visibility instruction still live in two places after `.2.3` and `.2.4` each corrected it by hand. `docs/book/src/introduction.md:20` still directed the reader to withhold publication until the ADR-001 naming gate closed, and `spec/governance/charter.md:30` still made single-owner accountability conditional on that same withheld state. Separately, `docs/book/src/roadmap.md:95` named `SIGNOFF-REPAIR.3.3.4.3.3.3.3` as "the current frontier", seven committed leaves after that leaf closed. The exact superseded wording stays quoted where it belongs: the preserved evidence record `docs/tasks/artifacts/signoff_review/publication-precondition.md`, and Git history.
- Root cause: both are the same failure mode, not two typos. A hand-run census is a one-shot instrument, and this one had already leaked twice (`.2.4` caught the CI guide). The frontier is worse: the book held a SECOND copy of a fact `docs/tasks/SIGNOFF-REPAIR.md` already owns, and nothing compared the two. The director reviews the book, so both defects land exactly where they are least likely to be caught and most likely to mislead.
- Owns: correct both wrong statements against the accepted director correction without reopening the decision; remove the duplicated frontier rather than re-synchronizing it; and mechanize both rules so a third leak fails a commit instead of waiting for a reader. No visibility change, no remote mutation, no new naming or release claim.
- Fix: `scripts/check_visibility_policy.sh` reviews every tracked-Markdown private-visibility sentence against a verbatim `.doctrine/visibility_exceptions.txt`, where a legitimate prohibition, negation or preserved historical quotation is listed with its reason; a NEW or REWORDED sentence, and a STALE exception, both breach. `scripts/check_book_frontier.sh` refuses a book page that names a frontier leaf other than row 1 of the tree's Current Frontier table, and passes when the page names none — which is the intended shape. Both register in `scripts/check_doctrines.project.sh`. `docs/book/src/roadmap.md` now routes the reader to the per-leaf maintained qualification page instead of holding a copy.
- Verification: `bash scripts/check_visibility_policy.sh` returns rc=1 on the unfixed tree naming both exact lines, and rc=0 after the corrections. Its `--self-test` fires on all four observed leak shapes and stays silent on four unrelated private-file/private-channel senses. A negative control that re-appends a fresh withholding instruction to the book introduction returns rc=1, and a deliberately stale allowlist entry also returns rc=1. `bash scripts/check_book_frontier.sh` returns rc=0 on the corrected book, rc=1 when the exact old claim is reappended (naming row 1 `SIGNOFF-REPAIR.7.3.3.3` against the page's `SIGNOFF-REPAIR.3.3.4.3.3.3.3`), and its `--self-test` verifies row-1 extraction plus two claim shapes and one non-claim. `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` prints `=== all doctrines green ===` across its thirteen registered checks, the two new ones running inside the `PROJECT-SPECIFIC` slot; `make book` returns rc=0. `git diff --check` first returned rc=2 on a trailing blank line this leaf introduced at `LIVE_STATUS.md:219`; REPAIR-0062 removes it and the recheck returns rc=0. The original commit's whitespace claim was wrong and is corrected here rather than restated. Documentation and check scripts only; no Rust source, production behavior or qualification category changes.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0061 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.13): mechanize the visibility policy and drop the duplicated book frontier`, then `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0062 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.13): correct the leaf's whitespace claim` for the `git diff --check` finding above. promotion: promoted → docs/decisions/2026-09-11_mechanized-document-invariants.md, three indexed answers.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.14 — Diagnose and repair the ordering-dependent site-operator refusal

- Status: `active`; opened from the source-165cb3a full checkpoint.
- Reproduce: the checkpoint's fourth command stops at rc=101 after 3,137 seconds. Thirty-six of forty suites started and thirty-five pass (259 tests); `site_authority` returns `9 passed; 1 failed`. `issuance_uses_database_identity_and_rechecks_membership_after_waiting` fails at `crates/reasonbraid-server/tests/site_authority.rs:610`, where an unprivileged database login must be refused `Error::OperatorRequired`. The same suite alone in a fresh owned cluster passes all ten. Four later suites, the demonstration and checkpoint gates five to eight never ran; the failed cluster is retained at `target/pg-tests/run-d4kvj1l5`.
- Forensics on a COPY of the retained cluster (the original bytes are untouched): the outsider role is a member of `pg_read_all_settings` only, and the member role is additionally in `reasonbraid_site_operator`. Role setup was therefore correct and this is not a mis-seeded fixture. The end-of-suite `site_boundaries`/`site_audit` counts are accumulated by the tests that ran after the failure, so they do not isolate the failing instant.
- Owns: make the refusal assertion report the value it actually received — `assert!(matches!(…))` discards exactly the evidence a diagnosis needs, and that opacity is itself the first defect. Then reproduce the ordering dependence with a narrowed predecessor sequence rather than the whole collection, establish whether the outsider was ALLOWED (a privilege-boundary defect) or refused with a different error (a classification or environment defect), and repair the proved cause. Preserve the retained failed cluster and every original assertion; no weakened privilege check and no skipped control.
- Acceptance: the actual returned value is named in the evidence, the mechanism is reproduced rather than inferred, and the repaired suite passes both alone and after the predecessors that trigger it. Whether the checkpoint then completes is parent `.11.4.3.1.2`.
- Root cause, re-derived from the catalogue: `migration_upgrade` recreates the database four times with `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE; CREATE SCHEMA public`. A fresh DATABASE ships `public` with a default ACL; a manually created SCHEMA does not inherit it. The failing database's `public` is `postgres|{postgres=UC/postgres}` while a pristine one on the same server is `pg_database_owner|{pg_database_owner=UC/pg_database_owner,=U/pg_database_owner}` — the `=U/` entry is PUBLIC's USAGE, and it is gone. From that suite onward every non-owner role in the shared database cannot resolve a qualified name. The probe `has_table_privilege(CURRENT_USER, 'public.site_audit', 'INSERT')` must resolve a qualified NAME, so it raised SQLSTATE 42501 and `operator_identity` propagated `Error::Sql`. `migration_upgrade site_authority` reproduces it deterministically in two suites instead of thirty-six.
- Not an escalation: the forensic copy shows the outsider held membership of `pg_read_all_settings` only, never `reasonbraid_site_operator`. The caller was refused throughout; only the classification was wrong.
- Fix, two defects: (1) all four recreate sites in `crates/reasonbraid-server/tests/migration_upgrade.rs` now go through one helper that restores both lost properties — `ALTER SCHEMA public OWNER TO pg_database_owner` and `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO PUBLIC` — so a fixture that mutates database-wide privilege state owns restoring it, exactly as the `.2.7` cleanup plans own their rows; (2) `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/site_authority/operator.rs` identifies the audit table by catalogue OID instead of a qualified name, so a caller without schema USAGE receives the honest `OperatorRequired` (HTTP 403) rather than `Error::Sql` (HTTP 500 `dependency_unavailable`). A missing table yields false, not NULL; the membership recheck, the freshly parsed simple-protocol statement and the absence of caller interpolation are preserved.
- Verification: the reproduced sequence `migration_upgrade site_authority` passes 4 + 11. The new control `a_caller_without_schema_usage_is_still_refused_as_a_non_operator` passes on the repair and FAILS on the unchanged production query with SQLSTATE 42501, so it is falsified rather than merely green; it restores the grant before asserting, and probes that the outsider genuinely lacked USAGE while the call was made. Three opaque `assert!(matches!(…))` refusal assertions in that file now name the value they received. `cargo clippy -p reasonbraid-server --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings` returns rc=0 in 2m11s and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` returns rc=0.
- Checkpoint duration, measured and NOT explained away: `02-check` took 3,922s against roughly 670s in earlier records. Reported compile time is 813s and reported test execution 127s across 97 blocks, leaving about 2,982s of wall time that neither reports. Nine rustdoc doctest-harness builds are the leading candidate and are not claimed as the answer. Concrete owner: `.11.4.3.1.2.15`.
- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0065. The affected family `migration_upgrade allowlist regions site_authority site_operator_cli site_registry_http` passes all six suites (30 tests) in collection order and its disposable cluster reports `stopped and removed`. The full checkpoint itself remains parent `.11.4.3.1.2` and has NOT passed.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0065 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.14): restore the recreated schema's grant and refuse non-operators honestly`. promotion: promoted → docs/decisions/2026-09-11_recreated-schema-privileges.md, three indexed answers.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.15 — Account for the checkpoint's unexplained wall time

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0090. The gate `.11.5` put in front of every new verification lane.
- Re-derived before measuring anything: `02-check rc=0 seconds=3922`; `grep -oE 'Finished .* in .*'` returns `9m 38s` + `0.46s` + `3m 55s` = 813.46s; the 97 `finished in Ns` blocks sum to 126.95s; 3922 − 813 − 127 = **2,982s (76%)**. The leaf's three numbers reproduce exactly.
- Instrument built and tracked, because cargo's own summaries cannot answer this: `scripts/measure_check_phases.py` runs each `make check` phase on its own clock and additionally splits the doc/non-doc phases that `cargo test --all` hides. Receipt: `target/check-phases/run-20260911T224102Z/phases.json`.
- **The leaf's own leading candidate is REFUTED by measurement.** The doc phase costs 310.5s of a 3,838.8s run — at most 8% — and 207s of that is rustdoc compilation cargo already reports. Nine rustdoc harness builds are not where the hour goes.
- The answer is `test-run`: **3,162.8s, of which 0.26s is compilation and 119.5s is the 88 harnesses' own time**, leaving ~3,043s spent neither compiling nor testing while 88 test binaries were executed.
- **Not inherent, which is the question the leaf asked.** The same commands on the Linux runner, from a COLD checkout (run 34652116508's `check` job, re-derived from that job's own log): 444s wall, 381s compile across **514** `Compiling` lines, 44.28s of harness time, **18.7s (4.2%) unaccounted**. The runner compiles 514 crates from cold in less time than this machine compiles 12 warm.
- Root cause (WHY + WHERE): macOS first-execution validation of a newly written executable, on the repository volume. A freshly linked binary's first run is `real 31.17 user 0.00 sys 0.00` — blocked outside itself — and its second and third are `0.00`. The same bytes copied to each volume and executed once: `/Volumes/SSD` 21.66 / 22.42 / 22.36 / 21.60s against the boot volume's 0.12 / 0.17 / 0.12 / 0.19s, ~150x, spread under 0.8s on the slow side. The cost is **fixed, not size-proportional** (81.6 MB -> 21.25s; 2.3 MB -> 21.6s), cached per file identity, and driven by Apple's own stack — `ps -axo comm` matches only `XProtect`, `XprotectService`, `xprotectd`, `syspolicyd`, with no third-party agent. There is no file-level lever: `com.apple.provenance` is kernel-managed, survives `xattr -c`, and clearing it changed nothing (21.56s against a 19.69s control).
- The one structurally untimed segment was checked and cleared too: the browser launcher's `version.log` is written ~20s after clippy ends, so its download/verify is ~20s, not the gap.
- Published model: `checkpoint test time ≈ (distinct newly-written executables actually run) × ~21.9s + the harnesses' own time`. 88 binaries predict ~1,930s against ~3,043s measured, implying ~139 first executions — more than 88 because suites spawn freshly built executables of their own. That residual is stated as a consequence of the model; the per-suite spawn census is **not run**.
- **The actionable number for planning: one more integration-test file costs about 22 seconds of every future checkpoint here, whatever it tests.**
- Deliberately NOT taken, because neither is a source change this repository can make and both are the director's: a macOS security setting, and the repository's volume. Changing a system security setting is not an autonomous act, and an untested lever must not be published as a remedy. Surfaced for decision rather than actioned.
- Decision: `docs/decisions/2026-09-12_checkpoint-cost-model.md`. Evidence: `docs/tasks/artifacts/signoff_review/checkpoint-wall-time.md`. promotion: accepted — `TOOLBOX.md`, "A slow gate is a measurement, not a verdict on the gate".
- No gate was weakened, skipped or reordered: the phase split runs strictly more than `make check` does.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0090 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.15): account for the checkpoint's unexplained wall time`.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.16 — Gate file termination instead of re-correcting it

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0066. Director asked for the call to be mine and to be signoff-grade.
- Reproduce: REPAIR-0061 introduced a blank line at end of file in `LIVE_STATUS.md`, its own acceptance record claimed `git diff --check` returned rc=0, and REPAIR-0062 was spent correcting both. Nothing in the registry checks file termination, so the same slip is free to recur.
- Root cause of the INSTRUMENT, not just the slip: the obvious gate — wrapping `git diff --check` — would be wrong here. A census shows `ROADMAP.md` uses trailing double-spaces as Markdown hard line breaks, so that check's whitespace family contains a legitimate use in this repository and a blanket rule would fire on correct content and teach bypass. A blank line at end of file has no legitimate use in any format the tree carries.
- Census before designing: 642 tracked text files, 0 binary, 9 non-conforming — 6 evidence artifacts ending with a blank line, 2 schema goldens and 1 benchmark corpus missing a final newline. Two of those would have been damaged by a naive fix. `crates/reasonbraid-core/src/envelope.rs`'s `write_schema_goldens` emits no trailing newline, so hand-editing the goldens would be reverted by the next regeneration — the writer is the defect, and the sync test compares parsed values so the change alters no comparison. `crates/reasonbraid-adapter/bench/v1/prompts.json` is hashed by `Prompts::digest` and published as `prompts_digest`, "the versioning anchor in reports"; a newline there moves that anchor and breaks comparability with every historical report.
- Fix: `scripts/check_file_termination.sh` enforces exactly one terminating newline across tracked text files, whole-tree so the CI backstop is real, with verbatim exceptions in `.doctrine/file_termination_exceptions.txt` for generated or digest-bound bytes and a stale entry treated as a breach. The six artifacts are corrected, the schema WRITER now emits the newline and both goldens are regenerated from it, and the benchmark corpus is the one reviewed exception with its reason recorded.
- Verification: `--self-test` verifies eight classifications including a Markdown hard break that must not trip and a binary file that must be skipped. Three negative controls each return rc=1 — a new blank line at EOF, a stripped terminator on `README.md`, and a stale exception — and the restored tree returns rc=0. `cargo test -p reasonbraid-core --lib envelope` passes 4 tests including `schema_goldens_are_in_sync_with_types` on the regenerated files. `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` prints `=== all doctrines green ===`. Documentation, check scripts and one test-writer only; no production behavior or qualification category changes.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0066 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.16): gate file termination`. promotion: declined (the durable rule is the registered doctrine and its mirror in DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md; it establishes no new cross-cutting fact beyond the check itself).

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.17 — Stop a coarse clock from naming concurrent guard fixtures

- Status: `active`; the source-5c8609e checkpoint stops at `02-check` (rc=2, 2,608s).
- Reproduce: `crates/reasonbraid-server/tests/pg_guard.rs:41` panics with `Os { code: 17, kind: AlreadyExists }` creating its fixture directory; the suite returns `7 passed; 1 failed`. Running the compiled binary fifteen times at default concurrency reproduces it once — intermittent, not deterministic.
- Root cause, measured on this host: `Fixture::new` names its directory `{pid}-{SystemTime::now().as_nanos()}`, and six consecutive `time_ns()` samples on this machine are byte-identical (`1789125006057405000`), so the stamp cannot distinguish concurrent callers. Three `#[test]` functions call `Fixture::new()` in parallel threads of one process, so two can produce the same name; exclusive `create` then refuses the loser and the `unwrap` panics. `scripts/run_pg_tests.py:356` appends `--test-threads=1`, which is precisely why this suite has always passed under the PostgreSQL runner and only fails under `make check`'s parallel `cargo test --all`.
- This is the third instance of one family. `.7.3.3.1` reproduced it in the production R2 input span and `.11.4.3.1.2.12` in the extractor fixtures, both named from a process id and a clock field. A name proposes ownership; only exclusive creation with a bounded fresh candidate proves it, and a clock is not a source of uniqueness.
- Owns: give the fixture a process-unique monotonic discriminator and a bounded retry on an occupied candidate, matching the idiom already established twice in this repository. Preserve every existing assertion, the mode-0700 and same-volume checks, and the `Drop` that removes the directory. No test-threads pinning: serialising the suite would hide the defect rather than repair it.
- Fix: an `AtomicU64` supplies the discriminator and the candidate loop skips an occupied name rather than panicking on it. The clock leaves the naming path entirely; `SystemTime`/`UNIX_EPOCH` are no longer imported.
- Verification: the repaired binary returns zero failures in sixty runs at default concurrency, against one failure in fifteen before — a four-times larger sample with none. `cargo test -p reasonbraid-server --test pg_guard` passes all eight; `target/pg-guard-controls` is empty afterwards, so `Drop` still removes each directory. `cargo clippy -p reasonbraid-server --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings` returns rc=0 in 1m32s and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` returns rc=0.
- Census of the family, run because this was its third instance — `git grep -n -E "format!\(.*process::id\(\)" -- '*.rs'` over the tracked tree, eleven hits:
  - repaired or already correct: the extractor support helper, `extraction_completion.rs`, `extraction_input.rs` and this fixture all carry a monotonic discriminator;
  - **production, evidence identifiers:** `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/snapshots.rs:335`, `claims.rs:241` and `derivations.rs:177` share one `uuid_like_suffix()` shape, `format!("{:x}{:x}", nanos, pid)`, which mints durable `snp_`/claim/derivation IDs. A standalone probe of that exact expression measures 8 collisions in 10 sequential calls, 918 in 1,000, and 269 among 400 across eight threads — roughly one distinct value per twelve calls. Concrete owner: `.7.4.1`;
  - **production, filesystem paths:** `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/git.rs:1164, 1185, 1262, 1305` build four ambient temporary-directory paths from the process id ALONE, with no discriminator at all. That is the R1 analogue of the R2 input defect `.7.3.3.1` reproduced. Existing owner `.7.2.1` already holds Git production/test scratch; the census is recorded there.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0067 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.17): name guard fixtures without a clock`.

##### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.7.4.1 — Replace clock-and-process-id evidence identifiers

- Status: `pending`; measured finding from the `.11.4.3.1.2.17` census.
- Sources: `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/snapshots.rs:329-336`, `claims.rs:241` and `derivations.rs:177`. A function named `uuid_like_suffix` returns `format!("{:x}{:x}", nanos, std::process::id())` and supplies the suffix for `snp_…` snapshot identifiers and their claim/derivation counterparts. It is not UUID-like in the one property that matters.
- Measured on this host with a standalone probe of the exact expression: 0 collisions in 2 calls, 8 in 10, 90 in 100, 918 in 1,000, and 269 among 400 produced by eight threads — about one distinct value per twelve calls, because the realtime clock advances far more slowly than the work between calls.
- Owns: establish what a collision actually does at each of the three call sites before changing anything — a refused insert, a replay path that adopts an existing row as "the same" evidence, or a silently misattributed citation are three different severities, and the answer decides whether historical rows need reconciliation. Then mint identifiers from the `uuid` crate's v7 generator the server already depends on, and add controls that prove distinctness under concurrency rather than under a sleep. Evidence integrity is the claim at stake: `ROADMAP.md` §12.6 requires every cited snapshot to link its own reference, bytes and receipt.
- Acceptance: the consequence of a collision is demonstrated, not assumed; the replacement is proved distinct under real concurrency; and any historical-row implication is stated explicitly rather than left to inference.
- Consequence, established from the schema and the call sites rather than assumed: `migrations/0028_evidence_snapshots.sql:16`, `0029_derivations.sql:9` and `0030_claim_assessments.sql:9` all declare `TEXT NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY`, so a collision is a key violation, not a silent overwrite — no evidence row is ever overwritten by another. What happens next is the defect: `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/snapshots.rs` maps EVERY storage failure with `.map_err(|_| SnapshotError::ReferenceMissing)`, and `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/api.rs:3402` maps every `SnapshotError` to `invalid_command`, so a server-side collision is reported to the caller as HTTP 400 "the reference does not exist" — a false statement about the caller's input. In the R2 pipeline `api.rs:1997` then does `if let Ok(snapshot)`, so the derivations are skipped while `outcome.acquisition` is still set: the acquisition reads as successful with nothing persisted.
- Historical rows: because the primary key refused every colliding insert, no stored row can hold another's identity. There is nothing to reconcile — only lost writes that were misreported at the time. This is stated as a consequence of the constraint, not as an audit of production data, which this project has none of.
- Fix: one `pub(crate) fn evidence_id(prefix)` in `snapshots.rs` mints `uuid::Uuid::now_v7().simple()` — time-ordered like the superseded shape and distinct by construction — and `derivations.rs` and `claims.rs` use it, so three copies of the defective algorithm become one correct one. The `uuid` crate with the `v7` feature is already a server dependency.
- Verification: two controls in `snapshots.rs` measure the property the old shape lacked — 8 threads x 50 mint 400 distinct identifiers, and 1,000 rapid sequential calls are 1,000 distinct, against the probe's measured 269 and 918 collisions for the same shapes. 92 server library tests, strict `-D warnings` server lint (1m11s) and workspace format pass.
- Routed, diagnosed and not fixed here: the storage-failure misclassification above is a second defect at the same call sites, of the same family `.3.3.4.3.1` repaired for grants ("a database failure does not prove that a grant exceeds its boundary"). It needs its own error variants and HTTP mapping across three modules and four call sites. Concrete owner: `.7.4.2`.
- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0068. The live `profiles` and `evaluation` suites, which submit snapshots, derivations and claim assessments through the real handlers, pass 31 + 3 with the disposable cluster `stopped and removed`.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0068 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.7.4.1): mint distinct evidence identifiers`.

##### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.7.4.2 — Stop reporting evidence storage faults as caller errors

- Status: `pending`; diagnosed under `.7.4.1`, repaired separately because it changes three error types and their wire mapping.
- Sources: `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/snapshots.rs` maps every storage failure to `SnapshotError::ReferenceMissing`; `derivations.rs` and `claims.rs` carry the same shape with `ParentMissing` and `SnapshotMissing`. `api.rs:3402` maps every `SnapshotError` to `invalid_command`, so a server fault becomes HTTP 400 blaming the caller's input. `api.rs:1997` then discards a failed snapshot with `if let Ok(snapshot)` while still reporting the acquisition as successful, so the evidence is silently absent.
- Owns: give each of the three error types a distinct storage variant that preserves the original SQLx source, map it to a safe 500 at the HTTP boundary while keeping the genuine missing-reference and missing-parent refusals as 400s, and make the R2 pipeline surface a failed snapshot rather than reporting a successful acquisition with no evidence behind it. Qualify each with an injected storage fault, as `.3.3.4.3.1` did.
- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0071.
- Census: ten `map_err` arms collapsed a storage fault into a caller error — `snapshots.rs:161,174,183,232`, `derivations.rs:90,103,123` and `claims.rs:108,124,150`. Where the query IS the existence check the genuine refusal comes from the boolean separately, so all ten were storage faults wearing a caller-error label.
- Fix: each enum gains `Storage(sqlx::Error)` with the source reachable through `std::error::Error::source`, matching the `GrantCreateError` contract. Their derives narrow to `Debug` and gain `#[non_exhaustive]`, because an `sqlx::Error` is neither `Clone` nor `Eq` and preserving the source matters more than deriving equality nothing used. The three HTTP handlers use the established `internal_with_log` idiom, so the cause reaches the server log while the wire keeps its safe generic message. The R2 pipeline's `if let Ok(snapshot)` becomes a `let … else` that records an `evidence_unstored` acquisition error and returns without an acquisition receipt.
- Verification: the new controls in the live `profiles` suite install a PL/pgSQL trigger that raises on `evidence_snapshots` INSERT, drop it BEFORE asserting so a failing expectation cannot leave the shared database rejecting snapshots, and then require HTTP 500 `dependency_unavailable` rather than `invalid_command`; a companion assertion requires a genuinely absent reference to stay HTTP 400, proving the repair did not turn every refusal into a 500. Against the unrepaired source restored from HEAD the control fails printing the defect verbatim — `{"code":"invalid_command","message":"the reference does not exist"}` for a server-side fault. Live `profiles` 31, `evaluation` 3 and `cards` 1 pass with the cluster stopped and removed; 92 server library tests, strict `-D warnings` server lint (1m35s) and workspace format pass.
- Honest limit: the R2 `evidence_unstored` path is qualified at the handler and storage boundary, not through a live successful acquisition, because that path has no live coverage at all — the census-backed gap owned by `.7.3.3.4`.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0071 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.7.4.2): report evidence storage faults honestly`.


###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.18 — Repair three dead book links and check the rest

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0070. Found by a read-only census taken while the checkpoint ran.
- Reproduce, from the RENDERED output rather than inference: `docs/book/book/cli.html` contains `href="docs/book/src/cli-state.html"`, and `docs/book/book/docs/book/src/cli-state.html` does not exist — the page is `docs/book/book/cli-state.html`. A census of the whole book finds 29 intra-book links of which exactly 3 do not resolve: `docs/book/src/cli.md:133` and `docs/book/src/cli-state.md:42,146`. The other 26 render correctly from the same pages.
- Root cause: the `DOCPATH` doctrine requires tracked Markdown to use repo-root-relative references so no checkout-specific absolute path is embedded. An author applied that rule to INTRA-BOOK navigation, where mdBook resolves a link relative to its own page. The doctrine's intent was satisfied and the navigation broke, and `mdbook build` does not validate links — so nothing noticed, in the surface the director actually reads. This is the same shape as `BOOK-FRONTIER`: a rule about the book with no mechanical check over the book's own integrity.
- Fix: the three links become sibling-relative, which does not breach `DOCPATH` — that check forbids absolute paths that reach into a user's home directory, and a sibling filename is neither. `scripts/check_book_links.sh` then makes the property enforced: every intra-book link must resolve. External URLs are deliberately out of scope, because a network call inside a commit hook is a flake generator, and prose references to repository files are unaffected because the book writes them as inline code rather than as links.
- Verification: the census returns 0 broken after the fix. `--self-test` verifies eight extractions — anchors stripped, `http`/`https`/`mailto`/`#` skipped, inline-code paths ignored, multiple links per line. The negative control reintroduces the exact original link and the check returns rc=1 naming the page and target; the restored tree returns rc=0. `bash scripts/check_doctrines.sh` prints `=== all doctrines green ===`. Documentation and one check script only; no production source, behavior or qualification category changes.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0070 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.18): repair three dead book links and check the rest`. promotion: declined (the durable rule is the registered doctrine and its mirror in DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md).

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.19 — Make a conformance refusal name its cause, and diagnose the first remote CI failure

- Status: `active`; the project's FIRST remote CI run, on pushed `a626768`, failed.
- Reproduce: `doctrines` and `supply-chain` succeed; `rust` (run 34603215022) fails at `codex_adapter_passes_the_conformance_suite` with `codex:lose: the certification refused: ambiguous_outcome_honesty: the lose trigger refused instead of dispatching` (`crates/reasonbraid-adapter/tests/conformance/mod.rs:57`). The same suite passes locally and `claude_adapter_passes_the_conformance_suite` passes on the SAME runner, so this is environment-dependent and specific to the Codex scenario.
- The diagnosis is blocked by an instrument gap, and that gap is the first defect. `InvokeOutcome::FailedBeforeDispatch` carries a `reason: String`, and `crates/reasonbraid-adapter/src/certification.rs` discards it at all four arms (lines 214, 245, 271, 321) with `{ .. }`, emitting a fixed sentence instead. The CI log therefore contains no cause at all. This is the same class repaired twice today — a refusal that names nothing — and here it is in the harness whose entire job is to explain why an adapter failed to qualify.
- Candidate mechanisms, none yet evidenced and none to be assumed: `tests/conformance/stubs.rs` names its stub directory `{name}-{subsec_nanos}` with `create_dir_all`, the same clock-as-uniqueness shape repaired in `.11.4.3.1.2.17`, `.7.3.3.1` and `.7.4.1`; `codex` and `claude` scenarios share scenario names, so they can share a directory; and on Linux writing a script that another thread is executing yields `ETXTBSY`, which macOS does not reproduce. The local tree holds 2,446 uncleaned stub directories whose timestamps end in `000`, confirming microsecond clock resolution.
- Census correction worth recording: `.11.4.3.1.2.17`'s family census grepped `format!(.*process::id())` and MISSED this site, because it derives its name from the clock alone with no process id. The census was keyed on a token rather than on the concept.
- Owns: make every conformance refusal carry the adapter's own `reason`, so the next remote run diagnoses itself; then repair the proved cause. No speculative fix, no retry loop, and no weakening of the six-box contract.
- Fix, in two clearly separated parts. **The instrument:** the three refusal arms at `certification.rs:245,271,321` now bind `reason` and append it, so a refused box names the adapter's own cause. The arm at 214 is untouched because there the refusal is the expected outcome and passes. **A measured defect in the same file:** `tests/conformance/stubs.rs` named its stub directory `{name}-{subsec_nanos}` with `create_dir_all`, which succeeds on an existing directory rather than refusing. Scenario names repeat across adapters, this host returns byte-identical `subsec_nanos` for consecutive calls, and the local tree holds 2,446 stub directories whose timestamps all end in `000`. It now uses a process id and an `AtomicU64`, the shape established in `.11.4.3.1.2.17`.
- **Stated precisely: the stub-naming repair is made because it is a defect measured here, NOT because it is proved to be the remote failure's cause.** That cause is still unproved. The instrument exists so the next remote run names it instead of my guessing between a shared directory, a Linux `ETXTBSY` on a script being written while executed, and something else entirely. If the next run passes, the honest conclusion is that the naming defect was the likely cause, not that it was demonstrated.
- Verification: all three conformance scenarios still pass locally (`fake`, `claude`, `codex`), the whole `reasonbraid-adapter` crate passes, strict `-D warnings` lint for that crate returns rc=0 in 54.16s and workspace format returns rc=0. The local pass is not evidence about the remote environment; that is the point of pushing and consuming CI again.
- **The instrument worked and the cause is PROVED.** The next remote run named it: `claude:lose: … the lose trigger refused instead of dispatching: failed to spawn \`…/target/conformance-stubs/lose-14317-1/claude\`: Text file busy (os error 26)`. That is Linux `ETXTBSY`: `execve` refuses a file still open for writing anywhere. With several conformance tests in parallel threads, one thread writing a stub while another forks to spawn hands that child the open write descriptor, and the exec fails. macOS does not enforce it, which is why this passed locally for the project's whole life.
- **My candidate was wrong, and the evidence says so.** The failing path was `lose-14317-1` — the pid-and-counter naming REPAIR-0072 introduced — so the directories were already distinct and the collision I repaired was not the cause. The failure also moved from the `codex` scenario to `claude`, which shows it is a timing race rather than anything adapter-specific. Recording this because the earlier leaf deliberately said "measured defect, NOT a proved cause"; that caution is what makes this correction a correction rather than a retraction.
- Repair of the proved cause: the two stub scripts branch on the prompt, so a per-scenario copy never carried any information — copying them was purely harmful. `stub_once` writes one stub per adapter kind per process and a `OnceLock` publishes the path only after the write and chmod complete, so there is exactly one write per process and no window; the process id in the directory keeps concurrent test binaries apart. This removes the race rather than retrying around `ETXTBSY`.
- Verification: `cargo test -p reasonbraid-adapter` passes every target (8, 7, 12, 2 and the doc target) and stub directories now take the shape `codex-433`, one per kind per process. The local pass is not evidence about Linux; the next remote run is. Residue noted and owned by the artifact review: 2,477 directories from the superseded per-scenario scheme remain under `target/conformance-stubs`.
- Verification / commit: REPAIR-0072 shipped the instrument; REPAIR-0073 repairs the proved cause. Remote confirmation still required — no CI-green claim is made from a macOS pass.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.20 — Retain the browser worker's own stderr in CI

- Status: `active`; the next remote failure after the conformance repair.
- Reproduce: run 34608054338 on `28515ee`. `book` and `pg-tests` SUCCEED — the full PostgreSQL collection passes on a runner for the first time — and the conformance suite passes, so REPAIR-0073 held. `check` now fails in `reasonbraid-browse`: six tests including `the_browser_renders_the_page_and_logs_the_network`, `a_real_navigation_deadline_stops_the_browser_and_origin` and `overlapping_workers_use_distinct_profiles_under_the_same_root`, with `{"error":{"kind":"browser_launch_failed","message":"browser exited before publishing a loopback endpoint"}}` and witnesses reporting `expected 1 gated navigations within 30s; observed 0`.
- What is already ruled out: the launcher reports `ci-browser: completed; evidence target/ci-browser/linux64-…`, and it verifies the executable's exact version before dispatching, so the pinned Chrome for Testing binary runs on the runner. `CHROME_ARGS` already passes `--no-sandbox` and `--headless`, so the usual Linux CI sandbox failure is not it. Chrome starts and exits before publishing its loopback endpoint.
- The diagnosis is blocked by the same gap as `.11.4.3.1.2.19`: the evidence exists and is discarded. The worker retains up to 64 KiB of `browser.stderr` alongside `owner.json` and `completion.json` for a failed or unconfirmed invocation, and the failing test even printed its fixture path — but `.github/workflows/rust.yml` uploads only `target/ci-browser/*/browser.json` and `version.log`, so Chrome's own complaint never leaves the runner.
- Owns: retain the browse fixtures on failure so the next run carries Chrome's stderr, then repair the proved cause. No speculative dependency installation and no weakening of the render or cleanup assertions — a guess costs the same CI cycle as an instrument and returns a maybe, which `.11.4.3.1.2.19` demonstrated when its candidate cause proved wrong.
- Fix (the instrument only): `.github/workflows/rust.yml` gains a `if: failure()` upload of `target/browser-lifetime-controls/**` — the worker's `browser.stderr`, `owner.json` and `completion.json`. The existing always-on setup-receipt upload is unchanged, and nothing about the tests, the worker or the render assertions is touched.
- Verification: the workflow's structure is checked locally (two `upload-artifact` steps, the new job named `browser-worker-evidence`), the thirteen doctrines pass and nothing in `crates/` changes — so no Rust gate is affected. The instrument's effect is only observable on a Linux runner, which is why this ships as its own commit; the next remote run is the measurement.
- The instrument's first attempt was WRONG and returned counts instead of bytes. The uploaded artifact carried only `worker.json` per fixture — `{"worker_exit": 0, "stderr_bytes": 403, "stdout_bytes": 108, "operation_error": null, "group_cleanup_confirmed": true}`. The harness writes its captured output as `stdout.log` and `stderr.log` (`tests/support/mod.rs:195-196`), but the upload globbed `browser.stderr`, `owner.json` and `completion.json` — filenames taken from the book's description of the PRODUCTION worker's `.project-data/browser/run-*` storage, not from the harness. I inferred the filenames instead of reading the code that writes them.
- What the counts already establish: the worker process exits 0 having written 403 bytes of stderr and 108 bytes of stdout, and the harness confirms group cleanup. So the worker ran correctly and reported `browser_launch_failed` as its own JSON result; the 403 bytes are the browser's complaint and remain the missing evidence.
- Corrected instrument: the upload retains `target/browser-lifetime-controls/**` and `.project-data/browser/**` whole, rather than named files. The fixtures are a few hundred bytes each, so there is no reason to filter — and filtering is what lost the evidence.
- Second correction, and the reason is specific rather than careless. REPAIR-0075's upload did retain `stderr.log`, but that file holds the HARNESS's receipts, not the browser's: `browser ownership: {...}`, `browser completion: {"render_succeeded":false,"cleanup_confirmed":true,...}`, `browser workspace retained: .project-data/browser/run-…`. Chrome's own stderr lives in that named workspace, and nothing under `.project-data` came down — because `actions/upload-artifact` excludes hidden files by default and `.project-data` is a dot-directory. REPAIR-0076 adds `include-hidden-files: true`.
- What each attempt did establish, so the cycles were not wasted: the worker exits 0 with `operation_error: null`; the harness confirms `cleanup_confirmed: true` and the process-group ownership holds on Linux; and `render_succeeded: false` with the workspace RETAINED, which is the worker behaving exactly as designed for a failed invocation. The defect is confined to Chrome's own launch, and its 403 bytes remain the only missing fact.
- A better instrument than waiting on stderr, and a real repair of a weak check. The director asked how the missing-runtime-library hypothesis could be confirmed or denied. Chrome's stderr reports the FIRST failure; `ldd` enumerates the whole population. `scripts/ci_browser.py` now runs an unresolved-shared-object census on Linux after the version check, writes `shared-objects.log`, records `unresolved_shared_objects` in the receipt either way — an empty list is evidence too — and refuses with the missing objects named.
- This also closes a gap the book had only conceded in prose ("Setup verification is distinct from real rendering"). `--version` parses a flag and exits, so it proves the binary loads and not that the rendering stack can; Chrome resolves libnss, libgbm, libxkbcommon and friends only when a browser starts. A prerequisite that reports `completed` on the strength of a version string is a check that cannot fail on what it claims to verify — the same shape as the refusals repaired earlier today.
- Local reproduction was considered first and is unavailable: no container runtime is installed (`docker`, `podman`, `colima`, `lima`, `nerdctl` all absent), and installing one is a heavyweight, unrequested environment change. The census therefore runs where the defect is.
- Verification: `python3 -B scripts/ci_browser.py --verify-only` returns `completed` on macOS with `version_verified: true` and no `unresolved_shared_objects` key, confirming the Linux branch is correctly skipped and the existing behaviour is unchanged. The census's verdict on Linux is the next remote run.
- **The cause is PROVED, and the leading hypothesis is DENIED.** With hidden files included, Chrome's own stderr arrived and names it: `FATAL:chrome/browser/process_singleton_posix.cc:313] Socket path too long: …/tmp/org.chromium.Chromium.d2KSCZ/SingletonSocket`, followed by `Received signal 6`. It is not missing runtime libraries. It is the Unix domain socket path limit: the path measures 228 bytes against a `sun_path` capacity of 108, over by 120.
- The 228 bytes decompose as the runner's repository root (42), the harness's per-command fixture `target/browser-lifetime-controls/case-<uuid>/` (75), the worker's private workspace `.project-data/browser/run-<uuid>/` (63) and Chrome's own temp directory plus socket name (48). Two nested 36-character UUIDs account for most of it.
- Mechanism, and why every local run passed: `crates/reasonbraid-browse/src/lifetime.rs:69-71` overrides `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` into the workspace's `tmp`. Chrome creates its singleton socket under the temporary directory PRECISELY to keep that path short, so the override defeats Chrome's own mitigation. macOS never reaches this code at all — `process_singleton_posix.cc` is the POSIX/Linux implementation and macOS uses a different singleton mechanism — so the defect is invisible on the development platform regardless of how long the local path is.
- Shortening names alone cannot fix it: reducing both UUIDs to eight characters removes about 56 bytes and still leaves roughly 172, well over 108. The repository root is also variable-length and outside the project's control, so any design that nests two generated directories under it is one long checkout path away from this crash.
- Selected repair, owned by `.11.4.3.1.2.22`: give the worker a SHORT repository-derived temporary directory for Chrome, and check the resulting socket budget at startup — refusing with a named error when it cannot fit, rather than letting Chrome abort with signal 6. A crash that the project can predict should be a refusal that the project reports.
- Verification / commit: REPAIR-0074 shipped the first instrument, 0075 corrected its filenames, 0076 its hidden-file exclusion, 0077 added the shared-object census. The census remains valuable — it closes a prerequisite check that could not fail on what it claimed to verify — but it is not what found this, and it should report an empty list on the next run.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.22 — Fit Chrome's singleton socket inside its path budget

- Status: `pending`; the proved cause from `.11.4.3.1.2.20`.
- Sources: `crates/reasonbraid-browse/src/lifetime.rs:69-71` overrides `TMPDIR`/`TMP`/`TEMP` into the per-invocation workspace, nesting Chrome's singleton socket 228 bytes deep against a 108-byte `sun_path` limit on Linux. macOS does not use `process_singleton_posix.cc`, so the development platform cannot observe it.
- Owns: a temporary directory whose path fits Chrome's socket budget, retaining §13 repository-volume storage and the worker's per-invocation isolation. Requalify the sixteen browser integration controls, and state plainly that a macOS pass is not evidence for the Linux path this repairs.
- The arithmetic decided the design before any code was written. Chrome's socket suffix is 45 bytes, so TMPDIR has a 63-byte budget. Measured candidates: today's absolute path 183; a short temporary directory under the existing fixture 132; a short temporary directory under a SHORTENED fixture 67 — still over. Shortening names cannot fix this at any reasonable aggression, because the harness gives each command its own fake repository root and the worker derives storage from it. Only a relative path escapes the nesting: 3 bytes.
- Fix: `crates/reasonbraid-browse/src/lifetime.rs` sets `TMPDIR`, `TMP` and `TEMP` to the RELATIVE `tmp`. The child's working directory is already `self.workspace.path`, so it resolves exactly where the absolute value did. Every other variable stays absolute, so the profile, cache, config, data, state and crash isolation the controls assert is unchanged.
- Why this cannot regress: if Chrome canonicalises `TMPDIR` it resolves against that same working directory and produces precisely the absolute path used before, which is today's behaviour. The change is therefore strictly no worse on any platform, and strictly better wherever the socket budget binds.
- Verification: the sixteen browser integration controls pass under the pinned runtime locally (31.27s), alongside the five production lifetime controls; strict `-D warnings` lint for the crate and workspace format pass. A macOS pass is explicitly NOT evidence for the Linux singleton path being repaired — macOS does not use `process_singleton_posix.cc` at all — so the remote run is the measurement.
- Verification / commit: REPAIR-0078; remote confirmation required.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.21 — Enforce the storage-locality policy that nothing checks

- Status: `pending`; opened from the corrected census in `.7.2.1`.
- The finding: §13 of the project's own policy states that all project-owned data must live on the repository volume and that tools must never default to an ambient system temporary directory or a user-home cache. Nothing enforces it, and `git grep -n "env::temp_dir()" -- '*.rs'` returns 8 sites across `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/git.rs` (6), `crates/reasonbraid-server/tests/backup_restore.rs` and `crates/reasonbraid-release-tool/tests/manifest.rs`. One is production (`git.rs:700`, owned by `.7.2.1`); the rest are fixtures writing project data off-volume.
- The second family in the same census: clock-derived fixture directory names with `create_dir_all`, which adopts an existing directory rather than refusing. `crates/reasonbraid-node/src/journal.rs:1330` and `:1780` (both after `#[cfg(test)]` at 1320) and roughly ten files under `crates/reasonbraid-node/tests/` share the shape repaired in `.11.4.3.1.2.17`. `target/journal-tests` holds 954 MB of their residue.
- Owns: a registered check that refuses `std::env::temp_dir()` in tracked Rust, with a verbatim allowlist for any genuinely required exception, in the idiom of `VISIBILITY-POLICY` and `FILE-TERMINATION`; and the repair of the clock-named node fixtures. A policy stated in prose for the life of the project and breached in eight places is the definition of a rule nothing checks.
- Note on method, recorded because it cost three attempts: censusing for a token drawn from the last instance finds the last instance. Census the CONCEPT — here an ambient temporary directory, and a clock in a path — not the spelling that happened to be in front of you.

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0085.
- CENSUS, re-derived at repair time rather than carried from the note above. Ambient temporary directories: 8 at the time of the finding, of which 6 in `git.rs` were repaired by `.7.2.1`/REPAIR-0082, leaving `crates/reasonbraid-server/tests/backup_restore.rs` and `crates/reasonbraid-release-tool/tests/manifest.rs`. Clock-derived paths: **18** across 16 files — larger than the "roughly ten" the note estimated — plus exactly one legitimate use, `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/authority/transaction.rs:111`, which measures a `Duration`'s sub-second remainder and reaches no path. 26 breaches in total.
- Both families repaired. The 17 remaining clock-named fixtures now take a v7 UUID and create the fixture leaf EXCLUSIVELY, while the shared parent stays tolerant of an existing directory — the distinction `.11.4.3.1.2.25` proved matters. `backup_restore.rs` additionally had a FIXED directory name shared by every run, with a clock supplying the only uniqueness in the file name; both are now repository-derived and exclusive.
- Why this mattered beyond policy: these fixture directories PERSIST between runs (`target/journal-tests` held 954 MB of residue), and `create_dir_all` adopts. With a clock measured at 501 distinct values in 2000 calls, an accumulating store makes collision with a PRIOR run's directory likely rather than remote — so a test could open a previous run's SQLite database and call the result a pass.
- Fix, enforcement half: `scripts/check_storage_locality.sh` refuses both patterns across all tracked Rust, with a verbatim reasoned allowlist where a stale entry is also a breach. `--self-test` verifies 8 classifications including a v7 UUID name and a duration measurement that must NOT trip. Registered in `scripts/check_doctrines.project.sh` and mirrored in `DOCTRINE_ENFORCEMENT.md`.
- Verification: the check reports 195 tracked Rust files clean with 1 reviewed exception; `env::temp_dir()` now returns 0 sites. Builds and tests below.
- Verification, measured: 60 node tests and the release-tool suite pass with the repaired fixtures, which now create 55 UUID-named directories on the repository volume; `cargo fmt --all --check`, strict `-D warnings` lint across the three touched crates, and all doctrine gates pass.
- promotion: declined (this applies the existing owned-storage and prove-the-name rules to a second family; the durable statement of both already lives in `docs/knowledge/proving-a-path-still-names-what-you-created.md` and the new `STORAGE-LOCALITY` gate now enforces them mechanically).
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0085 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.21): enforce storage locality and prove fixture names`.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.23 — Prove input identity against the descriptor, not an inode number

- Status: `active`; a defect in REPAIR-0063's own repair, found by Linux CI.
- The browser repair landed: run 34620840899 shows every `reasonbraid-browse` control passing, with `render_succeeded: true` and one worker reporting `stderr_bytes: 0` where it previously reported 403. `book` and `pg-tests` pass. The `check` job now fails elsewhere.
- Reproduce: `extraction_input::tests::a_replaced_input_is_retained_and_its_successor_survives` fails at `extraction_input.rs:431` with `a changed identity must refuse: ()` — `release` returned success where it must refuse.
- Root cause: `verify` compared a stored `(device, inode)` pair. **Linux reuses an inode number as soon as it is freed**, so a file deleted and immediately replaced can present the same pair. A standalone probe confirms both halves of this on the development platform: an open file reports `nlink = 1` while linked and `nlink = 0` after unlink, and macOS hands the successor a DIFFERENT inode (…892 against …891) — which is exactly why the defect was invisible locally.
- Severity: this is not merely a failing test. `release` deleting a file it did not create is the precise outcome the identity check exists to prevent, and the control was right while the implementation was weaker than its own leaf claimed.
- Fix: `OwnedInput` holds the open `File` for its whole life and `verify` refuses when the descriptor reports zero links, before comparing the path's `(device, inode)` against the descriptor's. An unlinked file still open reports zero links whatever number the replacement was given, so the check no longer depends on a number the kernel may hand out again.
- Verification: nine owner controls pass, strict `-D warnings` server lint and workspace format pass. The mechanism is proved by probe rather than assumed; the Linux behaviour it repairs is confirmed by the remote run.
- Still open in the same run, each needing its own diagnosis: four `git::tests::*` failing at `git.rs:1131`/`1205` (a `gix` commit, plausibly an ambient git identity the runner lacks — not yet proved), `crates/reasonbraid-extract/tests/support/mod.rs:142` which carries the SAME inode-reuse weakness in its `same_file` helper, and `state_store::unix::tests::writer_release_does_not_wait_for_an_inherited_descriptor`.
- Verification / commit: REPAIR-0079; remote confirmation required.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.27 — A render refusal was overwritten by an unconfirmed cleanup

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0089. Found by the first full run of the clean-state lane adopted in `.11.5`.
- Finding as reported: `crates/reasonbraid-browse/tests/browser_roundtrip.rs` asserts the response error kind is `time_budget_exceeded`; the clean-state run observed `browser_cleanup_unconfirmed`, with the budget preserved only in the message — `"time_budget_exceeded: the render exceeded the 30-second budget; browser stderr completion unconfirmed"`.
- **Two corrections to that report, both re-derived.** First, `group_cleanup_confirmed: true` / `cleanup_error: null` is the FIXTURE's `worker.json` receipt, which attests the harness observed the WORKER's group absent; the worker's own `browser completion:` receipt is a different fact and must have carried `cleanup_confirmed: false`, or `finish` would not have returned `Err` at all. The two receipts were conflated. Second, "it fails identically warm" did not hold: the unchanged control re-run here passed in 31.18s with `cleanup_confirmed: true`. The condition is load-dependent, so neither one pass nor one failure settles it — and that is precisely why the repair is not allowed to depend on observing it.
- Reproduce, DETERMINISTICALLY and without a slow host: the new control injects the exact shape the desktop-runtime diagnosis already proved (`browser-checkpoint-timing.md`: detached `chrome_crashpad_handler` and `GoogleUpdater` processes retaining the browser's inherited stderr write endpoint after the group exits). `R3_BROWSER_BIN` names a script that forks a child, calls `setsid` to leave the process group, and holds the inherited stderr; the parent never publishes a DevTools endpoint, so a 1-second render budget trips while launch still waits. Against unchanged production it returns, in 12.119s with no browser: `{"kind":"browser_cleanup_unconfirmed","message":"time_budget_exceeded: the render exceeded the 1-second budget; browser stderr completion unconfirmed"}` — the reported symptom, byte for byte in shape, on demand.
- Root cause (WHY + WHERE): `crates/reasonbraid-browse/src/main.rs`, the tail of `run_browser`. `Lifetime::finish` returns ONE `Result` that is the conjunction of four independent cleanup facts (process, handler task, network task, stderr task), and the tail used that single Result to REPLACE the response's `kind`, keeping the render's kind only as a prefix inside the human message. One wire field carried two independent facts, so the caller-actionable one was destroyed whenever the operator-actionable one was false. The budget is not special: `grep -n '"[a-z_]*"\.to_owned()' src/main.rs` enumerates the population, and **eleven** distinct kinds reachable once a browser is owned were being relabelled — `time_budget_exceeded`, `browser_launch_failed`, `browser_version_failed`, `page_failed`, `navigation_failed`, `click_failed`, `scroll_failed`, `type_failed`, `text_failed`, `title_failed`, `output_too_large`.
- The doctrine tension, settled rather than papered over: the rule is never to CLAIM an unobserved termination — it is not a rule to destroy the other fact. Carrying `cleanup_confirmed: false` obeys it exactly and loses nothing. The one corner where the replacement was doing real work is kept unchanged: a SUCCESSFUL render under unconfirmed cleanup stays `browser_cleanup_unconfirmed`, because it has no failure of its own to name and must not read as complete while a browser may still be running. Decision: `docs/decisions/2026-09-12_browse-refusal-carries-two-facts.md`.
- Fix: a pure `settle(render, cleanup)` combinator plus two new `BrowseError` fields, `cleanup_confirmed` and an omitted-when-absent `cleanup_error`. Additive on the wire — `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/browse.rs` reads the envelope as a `serde_json::Value` and takes only `kind`/`message`. The cleanup detail is deliberately ALSO appended to the message, because that spawner reads only those two keys and the message is today the only channel carrying the operator's fact into its log; both are written from one expression and cannot drift. No cleanup behaviour, budget, timeout or process handling changed, and no assertion was relaxed.
- The budget was NOT widened, and the assertion was NOT loosened to accept either kind. Accepting either would have cost twice: the control would stop proving that the deadline cancels a real in-flight navigation, and the product defect it was reporting would survive.
- promotion: accepted — `docs/knowledge/one-field-cannot-carry-two-facts.md`.
- Routed, not silently widened: having the server-side spawner READ `cleanup_confirmed` instead of relying on the message belongs to the spawner's own integration and is named in `.7.3.1`.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0089 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.27): let a render refusal survive an unconfirmed cleanup`.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.26 — Write every conformance stub before any scenario can spawn

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0086.
- Reproduce: run 34647830995, `codex:lose`, `failed to spawn …/conformance-stubs/codex-14645/codex: Text file busy (os error 26)`. INTERMITTENT — the same suite passed in run 34646239276 immediately before, so this is a race, not a regression.
- Root cause, and it is a correction to `.11.4.3.1.2.17`'s own record: that repair gave each adapter kind its own `OnceLock` and its comment claimed the result left "no window at all". That claim was false. One lock per kind serialises each stub against itself and against nothing else. `execve` refuses a file open for writing ANYWHERE, and `fork` copies the whole descriptor table — so the thread forking to spawn the CLAUDE stub inherits the open write descriptor of the CODEX stub another thread is writing, and holds it until its own `exec`. The failing exec was codex's, and the leaked descriptor was claude's write.
- Fix: ONE `OnceLock` holding every stub. `get_or_init` blocks all other threads until the initializer returns, so no scenario can hold any stub path — and therefore cannot spawn — until every write and chmod has finished. The window is removed rather than retried around.
- Verified by INVARIANT, not by symptom, because macOS does not enforce `ETXTBSY` at any timing and the failure cannot be reproduced here at all. The new control asserts that holding either stub path implies every stub is already written, non-empty and executable. Falsified both ways: restored against the superseded per-kind locks it FAILS — `…/codex-54781/claude is not written yet` — while all three conformance scenarios still pass, which is exactly why the race shipped; against the repair all four pass. Strict `-D warnings` adapter lint passes.
- The general lesson, recorded because it cost a second round: a claim that a race is closed is a claim about what CANNOT happen, and a suite of passing scenarios is not evidence for it. Assert the invariant that makes the race impossible, so a regression fails on the development platform instead of on the runner.
- **CONFIRMED GREEN ON THE RUNNER.** Run 34652116508 for `c17841c`: `rust` success with all three jobs green (`book`, `check`, `pg-tests`), alongside `doctrines` and `supply-chain`. Re-derived from the API rather than from a notification; falsified for hidden skips — the single skipped step is `Retain browser worker evidence on failure`, which is `if: failure()` and correctly does not run on a green build; durability confirmed, `origin/main` is still `c17841c`. The `check` job reports **669 tests passed, 0 failed suites**. The 8 `render_succeeded: true` against 5 `false` is expected — the browse suite deliberately exercises failure paths.
- **This is the first fully green remote CI in the project's history, and it closes the remote-CI repair sequence.** Each push in it cleared a real defect and exposed the next; none was a repeat: `ETXTBSY`, the 108-byte `sun_path` limit, inode reuse, `AuthorMissing`, a `pg_guard` check-then-act, and this stub race.
- **PUSH CADENCE RETURNS TO ~300 COMMITS**, which `COMMIT.md` requires this closing leaf to state explicitly. The standing exception — that while a remote gate is red each push IS the measurement — no longer applies. Pushes resume in batches after a passing full local checkpoint.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.25 — Stop racing for the guard fixture's parent

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0083. The remote `check` failure exposed once REPAIR-0080 cleared the git tests ahead of it.
- Reproduce: run 34646239276, `pg_guard.rs:33`, `called Result::unwrap() on an Err value: Os { code: 17, kind: AlreadyExists }`, `7 passed; 1 failed`.
- Root cause: `if !parent.exists() { create(..).unwrap() }` is a check-then-act. These fixtures run in parallel threads, so several see the same missing parent and all but one lose the create.
- **It was never Linux-only.** A probe of the exact shape lost 346 of 640 creations on THIS machine. It had never fired in a local suite because `target/` stays warm between runs, which makes the racing branch dead code; a fresh checkout runs it every time. Proved both ways here: with the parent removed the suite fails `7 passed; 1 failed`, the identical CI failure, and with it present the same binary passes `8 passed`.
- Fix: accept `AlreadyExists`, which is what every other shared-parent creation in the workspace already does. An existing directory is the outcome the code wants.
- Verification: six consecutive COLD-tree runs pass `8 passed; 0 failed`; the unrepaired code fails the first. Strict lint and the doctrine gates pass.
- Census: `if !<path>.exists()` guarding a create appears exactly once in the workspace — this site. The other `exists()` guards are worker-binary checks, and every other shared-parent creation already tolerates `AlreadyExists`. The UUID-named leaf creations are exclusive by design and correctly refuse a collision.
- Promoted to `TOOLBOX.md`: "remote-only" is a hypothesis, not a category. Two of today's three remote-only failures were reproduced locally in minutes by removing accumulated local state — the developer's git identity, and a warm `target/`.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0083 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.25): stop racing for the guard fixture's parent`.
- Correction, same leaf: the resume pointer marked `crates/reasonbraid-extract/tests/support/mod.rs` as carrying the REPAIR-0079 inode-reuse weakness. It does not — it holds an open `File` and compares the path against that LIVE descriptor plus `nlink == 1`, the repaired shape, and CI passes it. The weak form compared against a `Metadata` captured at creation with the descriptor closed. Measured on APFS: 0 of 300 reuses held and 0 of 300 closed, so this platform cannot discriminate the two — which is why the unsound shape survived here until ext4 exposed it. promotion: accepted — `docs/knowledge/proving-a-path-still-names-what-you-created.md`.
- Defect found BY that promotion, and fixed here: the LESSON-PROMOTION gate offers `docs/knowledge/<slug>.md` as a remedy, but the Knowledge Map generator scanned only `subsystems.md`, `docs/tasks/` and `docs/decisions/` — and `docs/knowledge/` did not exist. A record promoted that way was not listed anywhere, so it was not retrievable by question, which is the exact defect the gate exists to stop; the gate passed anyway because it checks for the artifact of the remedy, not its effect. `knowledge-map/scripts/gen_knowledge_map.sh` now emits a `Promoted lessons` section carrying each record's `answers:` line, verified by regenerating the map and finding the new record listed.

###### SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.24 — Give the git fixtures their own commit identity

- Status: `done`; REPAIR-0080. The last known remote `check` failure.
- Reproduce: run 34623064813 returns `88 passed; 4 failed`, all four `git::tests::*`, with the cause now named by the fixture itself — `the commit writes: AuthorMissing`.
- Root cause: `repo.commit` resolves its signature from git configuration, so four fixture commits silently borrowed whatever identity the developer's machine carried. A runner has none and `gix` refuses. These tests could never have passed on a clean machine, and passed here only because of a global config file that is not part of the repository.
- **Reproduced LOCALLY, which removes CI from this loop.** `env GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` reproduces the runner's condition exactly: against the unrepaired source it prints `AuthorMissing` three times, and it exposed a FOURTH commit site the first pass had missed. Recorded in `TOOLBOX.md` as a reusable probe — the first instrument today that observes a remote-only failure class without a remote.
- Fix: a `fixture_identity` signature used by all four `commit_as` calls, so the fixture owns its identity the same way it owns its storage. The email uses the reserved `.invalid` domain.
- Verification: with ambient git configuration suppressed, all six git tests pass; with the developer's normal configuration they also pass, so nothing regressed. The falsification is direct — the unrepaired fixtures fail under the same suppression. Strict `-D warnings` server lint and workspace format pass.
- Commit: `REASONBRAID-REPAIR-0080 (leaf SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.24): give the git fixtures their own commit identity`.
- Correction, same leaf: the resume pointer listed two further remote failures that the runner had already proved passing. Re-derived from the runs — 34620840899 failed five (four git plus the extraction-input identity test), 34623064813 failed four (git only), and `writer_release_does_not_wait_for_an_inherited_descriptor` prints `... ok`. So REPAIR-0079 is remotely confirmed and these four git tests were the only remaining failure. A "still open" list must be re-derived from the latest run, never carried forward.

The worker's `parent_digest` is computed over the file it read; the caller knows the digest of the bytes it supplied; nothing joined the two. `.7.3.3.1` had already reproduced the wrong-owner responses that make the join necessary, and `a_response_describing_other_bytes_disagrees_with_the_owned_input` in `.7.3.3.3.1` measured that the disagreement is observable at the spawner boundary — the missing piece was a caller that acts on it.
- [x] **FIX** — `extract_acquired_bytes` in `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/extraction_input.rs` performs the whole boundary: owned private input, spawner call, digest binding, release gated on the reader being finished. `ExtractionError::SourceMismatch { expected, received }` is a named variant, and because the enum stays exhaustively matched the api.rs handler could not compile without mapping it — it maps to the wire kind `extraction_source_mismatch`. The handler's span collapses from twenty-five lines to one call, and the adjacent spawner fixture owns its inputs too.
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `python3 -B scripts/project_env.py cargo test --locked --offline -p reasonbraid-server --test extraction_input` returns `7 passed; 0 failed` in 0.66s. `a_response_for_other_bytes_is_refused_before_it_can_be_persisted` drives a stub returning `sha256:0000…` against a real supplied document and receives `SourceMismatch` with both digests named. `concurrent_bound_extractions_each_describe_their_own_document` runs eight simultaneous callers through the real worker and asserts each receipt's `parent_digest` and chunk text belong to that caller — the property the superseded span could not provide. `the_bound_extraction_returns_a_receipt_for_the_supplied_bytes` and `a_named_refusal_survives_the_bound_extraction` keep the success and `feed_unreadable` paths intact.
- [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `env -u DATABASE_URL python3 -B scripts/project_env.py cargo test --locked --offline -p reasonbraid-server --lib` returns `90 passed; 0 failed` in 15.52s; `--test extraction_completion` returns `16 passed`; `cargo test -p reasonbraid-extract --all-targets` passes all 12 adjacent extractor tests; `cargo clippy -p reasonbraid-server --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings` returns rc=0 in 1m25s and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` returns rc=0. It restores the grant BEFORE asserting, and probes that the outsider genuinely lacked USAGE during the call so it cannot silently become vacuous.

## Commit acceptance — SIGNOFF-REPAIR.7.4.1

- [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — a standalone probe of the exact production expression `format!("{:x}{:x}", nanos, std::process::id())`, compiled with the same `rustc` and run on this host, returns: 0 collisions in 2 calls, 8 in 10, 90 in 100, 918 in 1,000, and 269 among 400 minted by eight threads. That expression was `uuid_like_suffix()` in `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/snapshots.rs:329`, with byte-identical copies as `suffix()` in `claims.rs:235` and `derivations.rs:171`, supplying the `snp_`, `asn_` and `drv_` identifiers.
- [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — the realtime clock advances far more slowly than the work between calls, so `nanos` repeats and the process id is constant within a process: the pair is not a source of distinctness. `git grep -n -E "format!\(.*process::id\(\)" -- '*.rs'` shows the same shape at all three sites. The consequence is established from the schema rather than assumed: `migrations/0028_evidence_snapshots.sql:16`, `0029_derivations.sql:9` and `0030_claim_assessments.sql:9` declare `TEXT NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY`, so every colliding insert was REFUSED and no stored row can hold another row's identity — there is nothing to reconcile. The damage is downstream: `snapshots.rs` maps every storage failure to `ReferenceMissing`, `api.rs:3402` maps that to HTTP 400, and `api.rs:1997` discards the failed snapshot with `if let Ok(snapshot)` while still setting `outcome.acquisition`.
- [x] **FIX** — one `pub(crate) fn evidence_id(prefix)` in `snapshots.rs` mints `uuid::Uuid::now_v7().simple()`, time-ordered like the superseded shape and distinct by construction; `derivations.rs` and `claims.rs` call it. Three copies of one defective algorithm become one correct one. The `uuid` crate with the `v7` feature was already a server dependency, so no manifest or lockfile change was needed.
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `python3 -B scripts/project_env.py cargo test --locked --offline -p reasonbraid-server --lib snapshots` returns `2 passed`. `evidence_identifiers_stay_distinct_under_concurrency` mints 400 across eight threads and asserts all 400 distinct, against the probe's measured 269 collisions for that exact shape; `rapid_sequential_identifiers_stay_distinct` mints 1,000 sequentially and asserts all 1,000 distinct, against the probe's measured 918 collisions. The controls assert the count of collisions in their failure message, so a regression names how many.
- [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `env -u DATABASE_URL python3 -B scripts/project_env.py cargo test --locked --offline -p reasonbraid-server --lib` returns `92 passed; 0 failed` in 20.57s. `RB_DEMO=0 bash scripts/run_pg_tests.sh profiles evaluation` passes `31 passed` and `3 passed`, exercising snapshot submission, replay, tombstones, the derivation graph and claim assessments through the real handlers, and reports `stopped and removed`. `cargo clippy -p reasonbraid-server --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings` returns rc=0 in 1m11s and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` returns rc=0. `api.rs` is unchanged.
- [x] **LOCKSTEP** — task tree, frontier, `LIVE_STATUS.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` and `DEV_NOTES.md` carry the same scope and the same limits. The storage-failure misclassification is diagnosed with its exact call sites and routed to the newly opened `.7.4.2` rather than fixed in passing, because it changes three error types and their wire mapping and needs injected-fault qualification of its own. The historical-row statement is derived from the primary-key constraint, not from an audit of production data, which this project does not have. Qualification categories and README are unchanged; no full-CI or push claim follows.

## Commit acceptance — SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.19 (the proved cause)

- [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the remote run on `8d3b35d` fails again and, with REPAIR-0072's instrument in place, names its cause: `gh run view 34606718559 --log-failed` shows `claude:lose: the certification refused: ambiguous_outcome_honesty: the lose trigger refused instead of dispatching: failed to spawn '<runner checkout>/target/conformance-stubs/lose-14317-1/claude': Text file busy (os error 26)`.
- [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `ETXTBSY`: Linux refuses `execve` on a file still open for writing anywhere. `tests/conformance/stubs.rs` wrote a fresh stub per scenario, and the three conformance tests run in parallel threads of one process, so a thread writing a stub while another forks to spawn gives that child the open write descriptor and the exec fails. Two facts make this conclusive rather than plausible: the failing path `lose-14317-1` carries the pid-and-counter naming REPAIR-0072 had already introduced, so no directory collision was involved; and the failure moved from `codex` to `claude` between runs, so it is a race and not adapter-specific. macOS does not enforce the rule, which is why every local run passed.
- [x] **FIX** — `stub_once` writes one stub per adapter kind per process, with a `OnceLock` publishing the path only after the write and the chmod complete. The two scripts branch on the prompt, so the per-scenario copies carried no information and only created the window. The process id in the directory keeps concurrent test binaries apart. The race is removed, not retried around: no `ETXTBSY` retry loop was added.
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — `python3 -B scripts/project_env.py cargo test --locked --offline -p reasonbraid-adapter` passes every target — 8, 7, 12, 2 and the doc target — and `target/conformance-stubs` now receives directories of the shape `codex-433` and `claude-98946`, one per kind per process, instead of one per scenario. This is a macOS observation and is explicitly NOT evidence about the Linux behaviour it repairs; the remote run is the measurement.
- [x] **NO REGRESSION** — the six-box contract is untouched: no check was weakened, skipped, retried or reordered, and the `RefuseMissingBinary` scenario still points at a path that never exists. All three adapters — fake, codex and claude — pass the same suite.
- [x] **LOCKSTEP** — task tree, `LIVE_STATUS.md`, `MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` and `DEV_NOTES.md` record the proved cause, and record explicitly that REPAIR-0072's stub-naming change was NOT the cause — the leaf's earlier "measured defect, not a proved cause" wording is what makes that a correction rather than a retraction. The 2,477 superseded stub directories are named as residue owned by the artifact review. No CI-green claim is made from a macOS pass.

## Commit acceptance — SIGNOFF-REPAIR.11.4.3.1.2.27

- [x] **REPRODUCE / ISSUE** — the new control run against UNCHANGED production returns `{"kind":"browser_cleanup_unconfirmed","message":"time_budget_exceeded: the render exceeded the 1-second budget; browser stderr completion unconfirmed"}` and fails at `browser_roundtrip.rs` with `left: String("browser_cleanup_unconfirmed") right: "time_budget_exceeded"`, in 12.119s, deterministically, with no browser and no slow host. The reported clean-state symptom is reproduced on demand rather than waited for: re-running the unchanged real-browser control here PASSED in 31.18s (`elapsed_ms: 31164`, `cleanup_confirmed: true`), so the load-dependent trigger is explicitly NOT the instrument.
- [x] **ROOT CAUSE (WHY + WHERE)** — `crates/reasonbraid-browse/src/main.rs`, the tail of `run_browser`: `if let Err(cleanup) = owner.finish(result.is_ok()).await { … return Err(("browser_cleanup_unconfirmed".to_owned(), detail)) }`. `Lifetime::finish` ANDs four independent cleanup facts into one `Result`, and that one value replaced the response's `kind`. The affected population is a census, not an impression: `grep -n '"[a-z_]*"\.to_owned()' crates/reasonbraid-browse/src/main.rs` enumerates every kind the worker can emit, of which **eleven** are reachable once a browser is owned and were therefore all being relabelled; `browser_missing`, `browser_storage_failed`, `step_budget_exceeded`, `no_steps` and `request_unreadable` return before any `Lifetime` exists and were not.

The success/unconfirmed corner keeps `browser_cleanup_unconfirmed` unchanged, because there the refusal is load-bearing rather than a label. Additive on the wire: `crates/reasonbraid-server/src/browse.rs` reads the envelope as a `serde_json::Value` and takes only `kind`/`message`.
- [x] **ADDRESSED (verified)** — measured before → after on the same control: `{"kind":"browser_cleanup_unconfirmed","message":"time_budget_exceeded: …; browser stderr completion unconfirmed"}` → `{"cleanup_confirmed":false,"cleanup_error":"browser stderr completion unconfirmed","kind":"time_budget_exceeded","message":"the render exceeded the 1-second budget; browser stderr completion unconfirmed"}`, 12.053s, `test result: ok. 1 passed`. FALSIFIED both ways: restoring the superseded collapse inside `settle` turns all three new controls RED — the pure pair reports `2 failed; 6 passed` with `an unconfirmed cleanup must not overwrite the caller's own fact`, and the end-to-end control reports `1 failed` — while the six unrelated controls stay green, so the controls are keyed on this defect and not on incidental state. The contract is asserted on BOTH hosts: the injected slow host, and the real-browser navigation control which now also asserts `cleanup_confirmed == true` with no `cleanup_error`.
- [x] **NO REGRESSION** — `python3 -B scripts/project_env.py python3 -B scripts/ci_browser.py -- cargo test -p reasonbraid-browse --locked --all-targets` passes **8 unit + 17 integration = 25 tests, 0 failed, 0 ignored, 0 skipped**, rc=0, against the pinned Chrome for Testing runtime. A freshly linked binary's first run is `real 31.17 user 0.00 sys 0.00` and its second is `real 0.00` — blocked outside the process, then cached. Same bytes on each volume, four pairs: `/Volumes/SSD` 21.66 / 22.42 / 22.36 / 21.60s against the boot volume's 0.12 / 0.17 / 0.12 / 0.19s. **Fixed, not size-proportional**: 81.6 MB -> 21.25s, 2.3 MB -> 21.6s. `ps -axo comm` matches only Apple's `XProtect`, `XprotectService`, `xprotectd`, `syspolicyd` — no third-party agent — and `com.apple.provenance` survives `xattr -c` with no effect (21.56s against a 19.69s control), so there is no file-level lever.
